# Project Ledger

This is the single authoritative production roadmap, decision log, risk register, acceptance register, performance budget, test record, and known-limitations report. It is edited in place. Evidence entries describe only observed results at the named boundary.

## Status at a glance

The 47/47 journey and save-isolation reports cited below are prior evidence from before the sustained case was added. The current local implementation-suite count is the later 48/48 result; its retained report verifies counts and messages but does not itself embed a Git commit, so clean pushed-source package and soak evidence remain separate gates.

A newer post-fix working-tree report created `2026.08.31-04.44.33` UTC passed 48/48 with zero warning, error, failed, not-run, or in-process entries; its report SHA-256 is `362487761b3b14e38fe15e1cb365e58de4ef8b677b6a4431910252deda98899e`, and its isolated before/after player-save manifests both hash to `03d8ae063da0b6d612e1451e7211e441b3d9a2a4f1c92ff439d1076c0303c512`. It supersedes the earlier 48/48 report only for current working-tree regression status. It remains pre-commit evidence and does not establish a clean pushed-source, packaged, sustained, or release result.

The project is transitioning from **Milestone 2 — Unreal playable-systems prototype** into the **Milestone 3 Glass Scar vertical slice**, with bounded work under **Milestone 4 — Architecture and performance gate** now measured. Version 0.93.0 retains the accepted networking, three source-reachable local skirmish factions, native snapshot/replay schema 23, and all fifteen bounded campaign operations through **The Broken Sun**, then connects the persisted records into one player-facing journey. `C` resolves the exact next authored mission from the validated ledger; success advances, failure retries, and a complete fifteen-record campaign returns to the title/archive boundary without inventing Mission 16. New Campaign and Restore are transactional across the active ledger and one retained prior generation. Replay conflict returns to the campaign journey without mutating either generation. Campaign-bound Mission 02–09 quick-save containers bind the exact prerequisite-ledger projection; unbound raw and version-1 bound checkpoints fail closed, while compatible legacy noncampaign raw saves remain loadable and are preserved during first container upgrade. The exact-source report created `2026.08.31-01.18.03` UTC records 47/47 successes with no warning or error entries. Its new isolated `FreshJourney` case drives two headless controller/simulation routes from an empty ledger through Mission 15—Preserve to Restoration and Harvest to Extinguishment—and verifies generation swap/restore and replay-conflict immutability. The subsequent save-isolation gate placed all 47 automation entry points under serial GUID-scoped temporary storage, passed 47/47 again, left the suite root empty, and retained equal sampled manifests for the real player SaveGames tree. The sustained implementation adds the protected-Core schema extension, strict package/evidence tooling, and the forty-eighth Unreal case; its latest gameplay-implementation report created `2026.08.31-03.43.17` UTC passed 48/48 with no warning, error, failed, not-run, or in-process entries and hashes to `434a2aa156eb04784cfbfe64235cd4bb86d6b3b3d00a7b59cb9035faa470858d`. This is implementation evidence, not packaged soak evidence. This does not establish rendered UI, physical input, ordinary-human completion, every route or ending, absence of a transient write/read, or broader persistence durability. The retained Mac-arm64 Development package is separately bound by its manifest to clean source commit `79d0cf3b45b7eeebed6d59a3939dae5ef0056421`; normal and 400-unit/four-team packaged startup smokes passed on this M1 Pro host. Its manifest and sidecar files hash to `de54024cd33fce495bc86fbadfbfbaa60808956dfa8f6372a6a9f3f151bd8540` and `83a817a2cc0236a5c6c99d048b12fe19174fd85fb6bc010d5507900471be413b`. Both current Metal profiles failed the 11 ms GPU and render-thread budgets. The 180-second memory diagnostic failed its slope gate, and the nominal 600-second result is rejected because the match ended before warm-up completed. This is a local ad-hoc-signed Development artifact, not notarized distribution or clean-machine qualification. Recovery tests cover logical single-process persistence/reopen and controlled API-level rename failures only; they do not establish multiprocess exclusion, filesystem crash consistency, or power-loss durability. The authoritative 8.2 GB checkout resides on the Seagate APFS volume with about 3.6 TiB free; the internal Mac volume has about 67 GiB free at this checkpoint and its former project path is only a symlink to the Seagate checkout. Remaining routes/endings, ordinary human full-campaign completion, cinematic endings, modeled downstream social consequences, broad three-faction balance/usability, final art/audio, passing current package performance/soak, complete multiplayer, and game completion remain open.

**Historical 0.90.0 boundary:** Version 0.90.0 at exact game-source commit `18ee3041f1e16d5a8c9c3b8099eef17f835a3499` retained the accepted networking and Missions 01–12 and implemented Mission 13, **Assembly of the Missing**. Its exact ordered twelve-record ledger retained 27 plans, schema 21, 27/27 content tests, 29/29 native suites, 43/43 Unreal tests, the twelve-record startup smoke, and the corrected Metal evidence at the limits recorded below. It did not make the Hollow Choir playable. That statement is retained only as historical provenance and is superseded by the 0.91.0 boundary above.

**Historical 0.91.0 boundary:** Version 0.91.0 at exact game-source commit `5fd15a4034f8502d65a4dc2634e545eb9ff65fc6` added the Hollow Choir's bounded systems, local skirmish selection, native schema 22, and Mission 14, **Several Voices, One Command**. Its 34/34 content, 32/32 native, 44/44 Unreal, startup, and corrected Metal results remain historical evidence at the limits recorded below. It did not implement Mission 15 or commit a final-resolution record. That boundary is superseded by 0.92.0 above.

**Historical 0.92.0 boundary:** Version 0.92.0 at exact game-source commit `083adf9b78287c0c7ee2cc6f927aa199ddd1cf39` added Mission 15, explicit ending eligibility, schema-2 ending provenance, and one complete automated plan-07 Controlled Stabilization route. Its 34/34 content, 32/32 native, 45/45 Unreal, startup, and corrected Metal results remain historical evidence at the limits recorded below. It did not provide exact-next-mission title continuity, the 0.93 transactional journey/recovery flow, exact prerequisite-ledger binding for Mission 02–09 checkpoint containers, or a current package. That boundary is superseded by 0.93.0 above.

The user deleted the first local project workspace during storage cleanup and explicitly directed that the game repository be remade. The current repository and authoritative files were recreated at the same path. The original local Git history and filesystem metadata did not survive that deletion; this limitation cannot be repaired by claiming continuity that does not exist. All subsequent changes remain in the current authoritative files.

## Decisions

### D-001 — Select Unreal Engine 5.8 as the production target

Use the precompiled Unreal Engine 5.8 launcher build and a C++ project. Keep deterministic game state in an engine-independent module. Do not build the engine from source unless a specific engine modification becomes necessary.

The project prioritizes high-fidelity 3D presentation, Metal support, mature navigation and AI facilities, profiling, automation, replays, and separate-process multiplayer testing. The user authorized and began Unreal installation. Unity is more comfortable on the inspected 16 GB M1 Pro, and Godot has a smaller storage/licensing footprint; neither removes the custom deterministic RTS work. Unreal must pass a measured Mac technical spike. Failure to meet frame-time, memory, build, or iteration budgets reopens the decision before content expansion.

### D-002 — Separate simulation from Unreal presentation

Commands, economy, combat resolution, Future Well state, AI-visible information, replay records, save schema, and match hashes live in a standard C++20 core without Unreal object, physics, navigation, animation, or wall-clock dependencies. Unreal owns input translation, camera, view actors, animation, effects, audio, UI, asset loading, and platform integration.

### D-003 — Use deterministic command simulation as match authority

The initial multiplayer model is host-authoritative deterministic command simulation with input delay, periodic state hashes, validated command envelopes, snapshots for reconnect/recovery, and a retained replay stream. Dedicated-server evolution remains possible. Pure peer lockstep makes recovery and cheat containment harder; fully replicated actor state does not by itself provide deterministic replay.

### D-004 — Optimize for the inspected M1 Pro without declaring it the shipping baseline

Use native arm64 builds, disable Nanite and Virtual Shadow Maps, evaluate Lumen software lighting only under profiling, and retain a conventional fallback. The M1 Pro/16 GB host is below Epic's M3/32 GB recommendation; meeting a minimum tier does not establish 60 FPS.

### D-005 — Treat Future Well outcomes as explicit state transitions

Harvest is irreversible after its telegraphed commit; Preserve is a contestable recurring state; Reshape is a time-bounded map-authored manifestation with deterministic expiration and displacement. Campaign consequences record facts rather than a moral score.

## Milestones and acceptance gates

### M0 — Environment and engine decision

- Inspect hardware, macOS, storage, Xcode, Metal, Git/LFS, engines, and workspace.
- Check current engine trade space against official sources.
- Record engine/source-build decision and limits.
- Separate interactive account/installer gates from automated work.

**State:** Complete for the technical spike. Xcode 26.6 passed local generation, compilation, automation, null-RHI/rendered startup, and Development cook/package gates; Epic's recommended 26.1.1 baseline, clean-machine compatibility, and representative performance remain open.

### M1 — Production baseline and simulation spike

- Isolated repository without credentials or machine-specific build paths.
- Authoritative design, architecture, setup, evidence, and asset records.
- Data definitions for both slice factions and Future Wells.
- Standard C++20 core builds with Apple Clang without Unreal.
- Tests prove deterministic command ordering/checksums, economy, placement/construction, combat, visibility, all Well modes, bounded AI inputs, replay, and versioned snapshot behavior.
- Failures return stable reason identifiers.

**State:** Substantially complete for the technical spike. Commit `adad96e` records the historical 7/7 baseline; the integrated hardening passes 18/18 in all three configurations. Four-player deterministic visibility, victory resolution, command sequences, schema-10 snapshot/replay state, and versioned authored-rule persistence are covered. Version 0.31.0 applies ten foundational faction archetypes and Future Well rules before match creation. Full-roster entity types, authenticated multiplayer-seat binding, replay work limits, and stable execution-time rejection reporting remain open.

### M2 — Unreal technical prototype

- UE 5.8 C++ editor target builds and launches natively on arm64.
- A prototype map exposes camera, box selection, context orders, pathing, economy, construction, production, combat, fog, victory, and restart.
- Unreal remains a view/controller over the tested simulation; frame rate does not change checksums.
- Stress scene records CPU, GPU, memory, navigation, and game-thread measurements.
- Menus do not advertise unavailable modes.

**0.93.0 update:** The fifteen authored operations and Mission 15 ending contracts remain unchanged. The new journey resolver opens the exact next briefing with `C`, retries failure, stops cleanly after Mission 15, makes New Campaign and Restore transactional across one active and one prior generation, and returns replay conflicts to the journey without rewriting the ledger. Mission 02–09 campaign checkpoints now bind the exact prerequisite ledger. One plan-07 Controlled Stabilization route remains the only complete automated downstream ending; alternate plans and endings remain incomplete playthroughs. All earlier milestone evidence remains bounded as stated.

**0.92.0 update (superseding the roster/count statements above):** The validated catalog drives twelve named units, twelve named structures, six technologies, and bounded mechanics for Meridian, Kharuun, and Hollow Choir. ### M4 — Architecture and performance gate

- Slice meets approved budgets or an explicit redesign is accepted.
- Path bursts, fog, formations, combat, transformation, saving, and replay seek are profiled.
- A 60-minute soak characterizes memory growth/cache behavior.
- Content multiplication is blocked until critical weaknesses are resolved.

**State:** In progress. The local optimized native scale harness measures a 64×64 Glass Scar-equivalent map with 400 units split evenly across four teams, 100 path requests, and 20 Hz simulation. Deterministic destination-field caching and the schema-9 four-player state/checksum design meet the recorded native p95 budgets; four-team visibility refresh is qualified at that native boundary. Historical 0.23.1 packaged Metal fixtures passed their recorded short-capture budgets. The current retained 0.93.0 package does not: both its 32-entity startup and 400-unit/four-team active-combat profiles exceed the 11 ms GPU and render-thread limits. Its 180-second diagnostic exceeds the fitted memory-slope gate, and its nominal 600-second result is invalid because the match became terminal before warm-up completed. The non-Shipping sustained workload, fail-closed validator/wrapper, exact package verifier, and focused/full automation are implemented. A same-package ten-minute preflight and uninterrupted one-hour active-workload run remain required before sustained acceptance; formation, transformation, save, replay-seek, network, and final-effect profiles also remain open.

### M5 — Multiplayer and replay

- 1v1 works between separate processes; 2v2 works at target scale.
- Version rejection, validation, desync detection, reconnect, disconnect, surrender, draw, lobbies, spectator state, and replay are tested.
- Loss, latency, reordering, and recovery are recorded.
- Separate-machine evidence exists before completion is claimed.

**State:** Not started.

### M6 — Content completion

- Hollow Choir, supported maps, technologies, AI personalities, 12–18 missions, cinematics/interludes, and ending logic are implemented.
- Every mission is traversed from clean progression and supported saves.
- Content/balance data remains synchronized with player information.

**State:** In progress. Fifteen bounded source-defined missions and four explicit ending types are implemented, with one complete automated downstream route through Controlled Stabilization. Version 0.93 adds exact-next-mission continuation, transactional New/Restore handling with one prior generation, replay-conflict return without ledger mutation, and exact prerequisite-ledger-bound Mission 02–09 checkpoint containers. Alternate complete routes and endings, ordinary-player full traversal, named or multiple slots, dialogue/cinematics/interludes, broader consequences, AI/content balance, and release-quality campaign presentation remain open.

### M7 — Release qualification

- Clean Mac build/install/launch succeeds.
- Campaign, skirmish matrix, multiplayer, saves, settings, controls, accessibility, performance, recovery, and rights pass release plans.
- Critical crash, progression, data-loss, and desync failures are resolved.
- Signing/notarization and distribution are verified if in scope.
- Remaining limitations are visible and accurate.

**State:** Not started.

## Initial performance budgets

These are the approved slice targets. Evidence entries below distinguish measured boundaries from targets that remain unqualified.

| Area | Slice budget | Measurement boundary |
|---|---:|---|
| Display | 60 FPS, 16.67 ms frame | M1 Pro, 2560×1440, medium preset |
| Game-thread simulation | ≤4.0 ms p95 | 400 active units, 20 Hz simulation |
| Render thread + GPU | ≤11.0 ms p95 | Slice map, representative combat/weather |
| Fog/visibility | ≤1.5 ms p95 | 400 units, four teams |
| Path burst | ≤6.0 ms, amortized | 100 simultaneous destinations |
| Resident memory | ≤10 GB after warm-up | Native development game, editor excluded |
| Match command traffic | ≤32 Kbit/s/client average | 2v2; snapshots separate |
| Save checkpoint | ≤250 ms; no UI stall >50 ms | Slice campaign/match state |
| Replay hash | Every 20 ticks, ≤0.25 ms p95 | Authoritative state only |

A 60 FPS editor view is not equivalent to a packaged native result.

## Risks

| ID | Risk | Likelihood / impact | Control |
|---|---|---|---|
| R-001 | Derived data, assets, builds, or packaging exhaust remaining disk | High / Critical | Stop prototype builds below 40 GiB; restore ≥60 GiB before large imports or release packaging; target ≥100 GiB sustained-production headroom |
| R-002 | UE 5.8 cook/package/rendered workloads fail with Xcode 26.6 | Medium / High | Retain limited positive evidence; install recommended 26.1.1 side by side if a toolchain issue appears or support alignment is required |
| R-003 | M1 Pro/16 GB performance is inadequate | High / High | Disable unsupported features, measure early, reopen D-001 if budgets fail |
| R-004 | Unit pathing/fog saturates game thread | High / High | Batched paths, spatial partitioning, scheduled visibility, flow-field spike |
| R-005 | Unreal state leaks into authoritative outcomes | Medium / Critical | One-way adapters, deterministic tests, replay hashes, review |
| R-006 | Reshape strands units nondeterministically | Medium / High | Authored transitions/fallbacks, expiry warnings, adversarial tests |
| R-007 | AI gains hidden engine information | Medium / High | AI consumes player-view snapshots only; disclose assisted modifiers |
| R-008 | Host manipulation or disconnect stalls matches | Medium / High | Validate commands, snapshots/hashes, reconnect, server option |
| R-009 | Art scope outruns engineering proof | High / High | Placeholder labels, silhouette gate, rights register, performance gate |
| R-010 | Campaign branching exceeds capacity | High / High | Bounded consequence variables and branch budget after slice |
| R-011 | Asset rights are incomplete | Medium / Critical | No distribution without retained license/assignment evidence |
| R-012 | Menus/docs imply incomplete modes work | Medium / High | Feature flags off, acceptance-led status, evidence review |
| R-013 | Workspace cleanup removes authoritative work | Medium / Critical | Current Git history, scoped cleanup, verify exact targets before deletion; remote backup requires separate authorization |

## Historical limitations through 0.90.0

- Unreal Engine installation and the Metal Toolchain are present; Xcode 26.6 has limited positive local evidence but is not Epic's recommended 26.1.1 baseline.
- The Unreal modules compile, link, load, bootstrap, and render a runtime-generated scenario. A complete skirmish and one automated route through **What the Ledger Keeps**, **Seven Accounts of Rain**, **A City on Reserve**, **The Unburied Road**, **Terms of Continuance**, **Names Without Births**, **The Shape of Silence**, **The Shape Beside Us**, **Reserve Authority**, **The Choir at Lume Reach**, **No Neutral Ledger**, **The Future That Won**, and **Assembly of the Missing** pass through the real Unreal subsystem. The demonstrated downstream route is specifically Harvest founding, Life Support plus Transit in Mission 09, and Preserve at Lume Reach; alternate plan contracts are not alternate complete playthroughs. Versions 0.58.0 through 0.89.0 establish the earlier bounded ledger path. Version 0.90.0 adds exact twelve-record projection into the same 27 plans, Oruun plus a distinct Kharuun verifier as the only commandable scouts, three neutral public mission interfaces, paired public readback, one ordinary Crownfall Listening-Spine link, paired assembly observation, thirteenth-record persistence, schema-21 save/load, and exact first-twelve-record checkpoint binding. The result establishes only bounded public readback, linkage, and observation. It does not assign responsibility or authorship, establish consent or trust, model civilian or survivor state, prove cryptographic authenticity, create mixed-faction command, or make Rhyse or the Hollow Choir playable. These remain bounded results rather than broad campaign/skirmish usability, balance, performance, large-formation, combat-cohesion, final-art, or release qualification.
- A feature-gated fourteen-operation title, operation-specific responsive briefs/objectives/results, explicit skirmish/prologue/migration/grid/recovery/continuance/census/listening/contact/allocation/Lume-Well/coalition-assembly/restoration-readback/public-assembly outcomes, deterministic replay/redeployment, recoverable two-press new-campaign authority, reversible two-press prior-generation restoration, active/prior record counts, field pause/options menu, camera, authored selection halos, authoritative selected/damaged health bars, context-order, construction/production hotkeys, pause/restart, HUD, tactical overview, non-color team markers, entity-view, Glass Scar and Lume Reach terrain, lightweight atmosphere, arena, lighting, placeholder fog/shroud, transient authored accepted-order mesh VFX, geometry-driven destruction feedback, registered command/removal audio, destination layouts, a contextual command deck, and edge-aware anonymous vibration cues exist. Version 0.65.0 adds inherited listening geometry, a relocated Waystone, ordinary Listening-Spine construction, paired memory witnesses, and a bounded confluence observation above the unchanged simulation authority. Version 0.66.0 accepts one exact-coordinate pointer path for selection, movement, placement, production, completion, and Future Well interaction and distinguishes interaction markers from movement. Version 0.67.0 accepts a visible swap between the active ledger and one validated prior generation while retaining the replaced active ledger for reversal. Version 0.69.0 accepts the authored selection/command mesh family; version 0.74.0 adds direct-Attack separation and one exact-coordinate controlled pointer combat/Guard integration path; version 0.75.0 extends that path through four bounded camera/HUD configurations with shared responsive layout and explicit non-occlusion checks; version 0.76.0 strengthens the gate to full actor bounds and seven fixed configurations over four resolutions and two aspect ratios. Version 0.70.0 accepts the destruction mesh family, fair removal lifecycle, checksum isolation, and standard/reduced presentation only at structural automation and isolated Metal-review boundaries. Version 0.71.0 accepts original command/removal cue provenance, loading, routing, rate limits, effects-volume control, reduced-dynamic-range behavior, and controlled live playback at structural automation and isolated Metal-review boundaries. Version 0.87.0 adds public Lume Reach navigation, claim-bounded proxy/contact language, and an exact-ledger save envelope without changing simulation snapshot schema 20. Version 0.88.0 adds neutral public district/evidence markers, claim-bounded link-near/evidence/rally language, and an exact first-ten-record save envelope without changing schema 20. Version 0.89.0 adds paired readback and district-input markers, a separate demonstration apparatus and Future Well, claim-bounded activation/observation language, an exact first-eleven-record save envelope, and schema-21 Well-activation timing with schema-20 migration at the simulation boundary. Version 0.90.0 adds public-record, Crownfall-index, and independent-observation markers; claim-bounded public-assembly language; and an exact first-twelve-record schema-21 save envelope. Views and overview markers remain scoped to the fair player view and explored terrain persists in the presentation, but mixed-faction allied command, final terrain/fog/UI/particle-effect/audio quality, unaided-human pointer usability, dynamic resizing, and camera/resolution/aspect-ratio coverage beyond the seven named pointer cases remain unverified.
- Source JSON is strictly compiled into a canonical digest-verified runtime catalog, and Unreal match creation fails closed when that catalog is unavailable, incomplete, or mismatched. The compiler covers factions, all named unit/structure definitions for both slice factions, Future Well rules, and four bounded faction technologies; map packs, ability schemas, localization, presentation references, duplicate-key detection at the JSON decoder boundary, richer technology graphs, and signed release manifests remain open.
- The core applies the loaded catalog to all sixteen named faction archetypes, Future Well economy/timing, seven bounded faction mechanics, and two sequential technologies per playable faction. Aegis connectivity is a deterministic Anchor-seeded graph through completed Power Links; autonomous fire requires powered state and ordinary visibility. Powered state, research, and the Mission 12 Well-activation tick are serialized and validated under the then-current schema 21; schema-20 snapshots migrate at the simulation boundary: dormant Wells retain activation tick zero, while non-dormant Wells conservatively derive activation from the snapshot's current tick. Mission 12 does not accept that derived value as continuity evidence. Final authored presentation, tuning, and balance qualification remain open.
- The current Adaptive AI consumes a separately materialized player-view value through a pure command producer with no simulation/world handle. Hidden entities are omitted; visible non-owned internals are redacted; unexplored terrain and hidden temporary passability remain unavailable; construction and cover placement require visible known terrain; anonymous vibration contacts contain no entity ID; and standard difficulty applies no bonus. Its five-minute command-core opening posture continues economy and visible-threat defense while suppressing vibration pursuit and keeping combat units near the core. Deterministic coverage and one rendered victory path accept that bounded behavior; they do not establish broad balance. The view does not yet retain general last-known contacts, publish the full scoped public-event/telegraph model, or implement a general `ICommandProducer` contract shared with human/network adapters. Current policies are not evidence of coordinated tactics, broader composition response, repair, multiple-base play, strong adaptive technology choice, broad balance, or human-equivalent skill.
- Native scale measurements qualify four-team visibility and other named costs only at the recorded optimized 400-unit engine-independent boundary. PERF-009 separately qualifies the exact 0.23.1 normal 25-entity packaged baseline; PERF-010 qualifies short-duration frame/GPU and resident memory for 400 visible unit proxies, 396 broad combat orders, actual damage, accessible feedback, tactical overview, and lightweight atmosphere. It omits the current command effects and formation controller and does not prove networked play, sustained current-workload stability, player experience, or final visual fidelity. SOAK-001 applies only to the older 0.15.0 stationary proxy fixture.
- No final environment or complete audio family is registered. A first project-authored static-mesh candidate is registered for each of the sixteen current faction units and structures; the Future Well has a four-part authored landmark candidate; and the Glass Scar has authored shelf, ridge, shard, Ash Cut, Buried Causeway, Folded Verge, and Matter-deposit candidates plus a shared world material. Versions 0.68.0, 0.72.0, and 0.73.0 advance all three routes through dedicated production-oriented topology, UV, material, collision, provenance, regeneration, runtime-isolation, and isolated-review gates. Version 0.74.0 registers one authored selection halo, seven accepted-order sigils including distinct direct Attack and Attack-move assets, one orbit accent, and one emissive material with bounded standard/reduced behavior. Version 0.70.0 registers ring, core, and shard destruction-state meshes with bounded standard/reduced behavior after fair authoritative removal. Version 0.71.0 registers three deterministic command/removal SoundWave candidates with bounded 2D/spatial, throttling, effects-volume, and reduced-dynamic-range behavior. These do not establish production textures, final surface response, character animation, transparent dissolve, Niagara debris/smoke, music, voice, ambience, complete alerts, broad simultaneous-combat readability or mixing, package performance, or final visual/audio qualification. Collision floor, fog, atmosphere, formation controllers, health/ownership cues, and HUD presentation still rely on code-authored or Engine-provided prototype presentation.
- Thirteen campaign missions and thirteen durable consequence records are implemented. Each later mission requires the earlier ledger to remain ordered and choice-consistent. Mission 07 through Mission 12 retain their previously accepted correspondence, reciprocal-contact, local-allocation, public-contact, public-ledger, and protocol/readback boundaries. Mission 13 consumes the exact first twelve records through paired public-record readback, one ordinary local Listening-Spine link near the Crownfall index, and paired assembly observation. Its thirteenth record means only that the bounded public readback/link/observation contract completed; it does not encode responsibility, authorship, consent, trust, civilian or survivor state, cryptographic authenticity, or mixed-faction command. The only complete automated downstream route is Harvest founding, Life Support plus Transit, and Preserve at Lume Reach; alternate Mission 09 through Mission 13 plans have plan/reducer coverage rather than complete playthroughs. A new campaign can replace the active ledger after an explicit two-press warning while retaining one prior generation; the title can validate and reversibly activate that generation through its own two-press warning. There is no named or multiple-slot browser, arbitrary history, selective record merge, or cryptographically authenticated local save. Missions 14–15, broader map/graph progression, save slots, downstream relationship/civilian/technology effects, multiple endings, dialogue/cinematics, difficulty progression, and broad campaign balance remain design only.
- Fourteen feature-gated single-player title operations are implemented: the two-faction Glass Scar skirmish plus the thirteen named campaign missions through **Assembly of the Missing**. A general player-facing multiplayer title flow, replay, broader skirmish setup UI, named/multiple campaign-slot and general save/load selection UI, remapping, subtitles, separate music/dialogue/interface/ambience buses, complete accessibility menus, and autosave are not implemented. The title, operation/faction choice, recoverable new-campaign confirmation, reversible one-generation campaign restoration, operation-specific brief/objectives/results, field pause/options menu, result/redeployment flow, persistent HUD scale, high contrast, reduced motion, reduced flashing, edge pan, pan speed, zoom step, effects volume, reduced dynamic range, offline pause, operation-isolated quick-save recovery, non-color ownership markers, and tactical overview are implemented as a bounded subset.
- Both slice factions are source-reachable and null-RHI integration-tested as the local player. Prior Metal gates accept predeployment faction selection, both technology archives, controlled research completion/interruption, live Tab/Backspace owned-entity traversal, controlled anonymous-contact presentation, and ordinary F3 Waystone migration. ## Current known limitations

- Fifteen campaign missions and fifteen ordered consequence records are implemented. Existing focused coverage retains the Harvest-founding, Life-Support-plus-Transit, Preserve-at-Lume-Reach, plan-07 Controlled Stabilization route. The isolated `FreshJourney` acceptance additionally completes Preserve/Preserve to Restoration and Harvest/Harvest to Extinguishment from empty ledgers through Mission 15. Version 0.93 can continue a valid campaign to the exact next operation, retry a failed operation, and stop at the completed journey boundary, but this remains headless controller/simulation automation rather than rendered UI, physical input, or an ordinary-human full-campaign traversal. The other plan combinations and two remaining endings have plan, eligibility, reducer, and record coverage rather than complete end-to-end route evidence. Broad branching, final dialogue, cinematics, and downstream relationship/civilian consequences remain open.
- Sixteen feature-gated single-player title operations are implemented: the Glass Scar skirmish plus fifteen campaign missions through **The Broken Sun**. Meridian, Kharuun, and Hollow Choir are source-reachable local skirmish selections with complete source-defined slice rosters and two technologies each. This does not establish broad three-faction balance, every matchup, unaided-human usability, or final faction presentation.
- Mission 15 implements one bounded final-accord and ending contract. Its record proves only that the exact approach, witnessed accord, selected conduit, hold, protected-force/witness, and local-Core contract completed and that the named ending was saved. It does not model the ending's downstream social effects, make unchosen endings occur, prove a canonical moral answer, or turn the prototype chain into a release-quality campaign.
- Native snapshot/replay schema 23 persists Hollow Choir identity/coherence state and the protected-player Command Core mask used only by the sustained fixture; schemas 20 through 22 remain bounded migration inputs, and current replay import requires schema 23. Campaign schema 2 adds separate final-resolution, availability-mask, and plan-key provenance while migrating schema-1 ledgers. Mission 15's `ECHOM15Q` version-2 envelope binds its pending/selected ending, irreversible failure latch, exact schema-2 Mission 01–14 projection, structure IDs, and native snapshot under one CRC32. Mission 02–09 campaign checkpoint containers additionally bind the exact prerequisite-ledger projection. Unbound raw and version-1 bound campaign checkpoints fail closed; a compatible legacy noncampaign raw save remains loadable and is preserved as the prior generation during first container upgrade. CRC integrity rejects accidental corruption but is not cryptographic authentication against an actor able to forge a complete envelope. Campaign storage still provides only active plus one prior generation, not named or multiple slots, arbitrary history, selective merge, or authenticated saves. Recovery testing covers controlled single-process/API-level rename failures only; it does not establish multiprocess exclusion, filesystem-wide crash consistency, or power-loss durability.
- Version 0.93.0 has a current 48/48 local Unreal automation report, focused startup/title evidence, and a later retained Mac-arm64 Development package exactly bound to clean source commit `dab16f281b9fe3fe84f463b556fe23dd56bd36ca`. That package passes normal and four-team null-RHI startup, its local ad-hoc seal verifies, and two local packaged Metal captures accept the specific world-surface instancing repair. The ordinary capture also exposes unresolved overexposure and debug-like overlay debt. Performance has not been rerun on this later package. The earlier package at `79d0cf3b45b7eeebed6d59a3939dae5ef0056421` failed normal/stress GPU and render-thread budgets; its 180-second memory diagnostic failed the slope budget; and its nominal 600-second result is rejected because the match became terminal before warm-up. Version 0.93.0 still has no accepted sustained current-workload soak, Developer ID signature, notarization, installer, clean-machine result, or release qualification.
- The authoritative 8.2 GB checkout resides at `/Volumes/Seagate Game Archive/EchoesOfTheBrokenSun/Project`, where the APFS volume has about 3.6 TiB free. The internal Mac volume has about 67 GiB free; `/Users/angelispseftis/PycharmProjects/echoes-of-the-broken-sun` is only a symlink to the external checkout, not a duplicate. The Seagate volume must remain mounted; internal engine caches and temporary build products still require space discipline.
- Multiplayer remains bounded to the accepted in-process/local-host authority, reconnect, delivery, and non-shipping NetDriver packet-profile evidence. Operating-system traffic conditioning, separate processes or machines, authenticated identity, protected credentials, encryption, private invitations, spectators, matchmaking, hostile-traffic and abuse qualification, security review, and representative complete matches remain open.
- Final terrain, textures, animation, fog, effects, voice, physical-human pointer usability, arbitrary/live-resize layout coverage, broad simultaneous-combat readability, final asset-rights qualification, signed/notarized distribution, and game completion remain open. As of 2026-09-01, 54 registered synthesized cues (interface, alerts, gameplay, music, ambience) exist as byte-idempotent sources and imported SoundWaves behind a five-category submix graph with per-category volumes and whole-graph reduced dynamic range, and music/ambience runtime managers are wired to title, deployment, and result flow; gameplay/interface event routing, rendered playback, loudness targets, and mix qualification remain open.
